   12|       | * Slide a selected window over n positions.
   13|       | *
   14|       | * Empty space is filled by shifting existing elements outside the window,
   15|       | * like dragging and dropping a contiguous selection up or down a list.
   16|       | *
   17|       | * @param first front of sliding window
   18|       | * @param last back of sliding window
   19|       | * @param amount how far to slide, left if negative and right if positive
   20|       | * @tparam RandomIt random access iterator
   21|       | * @return new position of selection window front
   22|       | */
   23|       |template<typename RandomIt>
   24|  1.00k|RandomIt slide_n(RandomIt first, RandomIt last, const int amount) {/*_FORCE_COVER_START_*/
   25|       |
   26|  1.00k|  const RandomIt to = (amount > 0)
   27|  1.00k|    ? last + amount
   28|  1.00k|    : first + amount
   29|  1.00k|  ;
   30|  1.00k|  return sgpl::slide_to(first, last, to);
   31|       |
   32|  1.00k|}/*_FORCE_COVER_END_*/

    6|       |#include <cassert>
    7|       |
    8|       |namespace sgpl {
    9|       |
   10|       |/**
   11|       | * Move the a selected range to a target position.
   12|       | *
   13|       | * The front of the selected range is repositioned so as if inserted before the
   14|       | * target position. If the target position is within the selected range this
   15|       | * will result in a no-op. In the case of an empty selected range, the target
   16|       | * position is returned.
   17|       | *
   18|       | * @param first front of selection
   19|       | * @param last back of selection
   20|       | * @param to target to slide to
   21|       | * @tparam random access iterator type
   22|       | * @return iterator at the new position of the front of the selected range
   23|       | */
   24|       |template<typename RandomIt>
   25|  1.00k|RandomIt slide_to(const RandomIt first, const RandomIt last, const RandomIt to) {/*_FORCE_COVER_START_*/
   26|       |
   27|  1.00k|  assert( first <= last );
   28|       |
   29|       |  // adapted from https://youtu.be/W2tWOdzgXHA?t=778
   30|       |  // slide backward
   31|  1.00k|  if (to < first) {
   32|    450|    std::rotate(to, first, last);
   33|    450|    return to;
   34|    450|  }
   35|       |  // slide forward
   36|    550|  else if (last < to) return std::rotate(first, last, to);
   37|       |  // slide inside selected window
   38|    126|  else return first;
   39|       |
   40|  1.00k|}/*_FORCE_COVER_END_*/
   41|       |
   42|       |} // namespace sgpl
   43|       |
   44|       |#endif // #ifndef SGPL_ALGORITHM_SLIDE_TO_HPP_INCLUDE

   13|       |
   14|       |class ThreadLocalRandom {
   15|       |
   16|       |  uit_emp::Random rand{ 1 };
   17|       |
   18|       |  uint32_t cache;
   19|       |
   20|       |  std::byte* cache_ptr{
   21|       |    reinterpret_cast<std::byte*>( &cache )
   22|       |  };
   23|       |
   24|       |  size_t cache_pos{ sizeof(cache) };
   25|       |
   26|       |public:
   27|       |
   28|  4.00k|  uit_emp::Random& Get() { return rand; }
   29|       |
   30|      0|  std::byte GetByte() {
   31|      0|
   32|      0|    if ( cache_pos == sizeof( cache ) ) {
   33|      0|      cache_pos %= sizeof( cache );
   34|      0|      cache = Get().GetUInt();
   35|      0|    }
   36|      0|
   37|      0|    const auto res { cache_ptr[cache_pos] };
   38|      0|    ++cache_pos;
   39|      0|    return res;
   40|      0|
   41|      0|  }
   42|       |
   43|      1|  void Reseed( const int seed ) {
   44|       |
   45|       |    // seed <= 0 non-deterministic (uses system time and memory address)
   46|      1|    assert( seed > 0 );
   47|       |
   48|      1|    cache_pos = sizeof( cache );
   49|       |
   50|      1|    Get() = uit_emp::Random{ seed };
   51|       |
   52|      1|  }
   53|       |
   54|      0|  void Initialize( const int seed ) {
   55|      0|
   56|      0|    // assert that rng hasn't been touched already
   57|      0|    assert( Get().GetUInt() == uit_emp::Random{ 1 }.GetUInt() );
   58|      0|
   59|      0|    Reseed( seed );
   60|      0|
   61|      0|  }
   62|       |
   63|      0|  void SeedStochastically() { Get() = uit_emp::Random{ -1 }; }
   64|       |

    1|       |/**
    2|       | *  @note This file is part of Empirical, https://github.com/devosoft/Empirical
    3|       | *  @copyright Copyright (C) Michigan State University, MIT Software license; see doc/LICENSE.md
    4|       | *  @date 2020.
    5|       | *
    6|       | *  @file always_assert.hpp
    7|       | *  @brief A more dynamic replacement for standard library asserts.
    8|       | *  @note Status: RELEASE
    9|       | *
   10|       | *  A replacement for the system-level assert.h, called "emp_always_assert"
   11|       | *  Added functionality:
   12|       | *   - If compiled with Emscripten, will provide pop-up alerts in a web browser.
   13|       | *   - emp_assert can take additional arguments.  If the assert is triggered,
   14|       | *     those extra arguments will be evaluated and printed.
   15|       | *   - if EMP_TDEBUG is defined, emp_assert() goes into test mode and records
   16|       | *     failures, but does not abort.  (useful for unit tests of asserts)
   17|       | *
   18|       | *  Example:
   19|       | *
   20|       | *     int a = 6;
   21|       | *     emp_always_assert(a==5, a);
   22|       | *
   23|       | *  Unlinke "emp_assert", "emp_always_assert" will trigger an assertion error
   24|       | *  whether compiled in debug mode or not.
   25|       | *
   26|       | */
   27|       |
   28|       |#ifndef EMP_ALWAYS_ASSERT_HPP
   29|       |#define EMP_ALWAYS_ASSERT_HPP
   30|       |
   31|       |#include <cstdlib>
   32|       |
   33|       |#include "_assert_trigger.hpp"
   34|       |#include "EMP_NAMESPACE.hpp"
   35|       |#include "macros.hpp"
   36|       |
   37|       |/// Helper macro used throughout...
   38|      0|#define emp_assert_TO_PAIR(X) EMP_STRINGIFY(X) , X
   39|       |
   40|       |#if defined( __EMSCRIPTEN__ )
   41|       |
   42|      0|  #define emp_always_assert_impl(...)                                          \
   43|      0|    do {                                                                       \
   44|      0|      !(EMP_GET_ARG_1(__VA_ARGS__, ~))                                         \
   45|      0|      && EMP_NAMESPACE()::assert_trigger(                                      \
   46|      0|        __FILE__, __LINE__,                                                    \
   47|      0|        EMP_STRINGIFY( EMP_GET_ARG_1(__VA_ARGS__, ~),  ),                      \
   48|      0|        EMP_WRAP_ARGS(emp_assert_TO_PAIR, __VA_ARGS__)                         \
   49|      0|      );                                                                       \
   50|      0|    } while(0)
   51|       |
   52|       |#elif defined( _MSC_VER )
   53|       |
   54|      0|  #define emp_always_assert_msvc_impl(TEST)                                    \
   55|      0|    do {                                                                       \
   56|      0|      !(TEST)                                                                  \
   57|      0|      && EMP_NAMESPACE()::assert_trigger(LE__, __LINE__, #TEST, 0)             \
   58|      0|      && (std::abort(), false);                                                \
   59|      0|    } while(0)
   60|       |
   61|      0|  #define emp_always_assert_impl(TEST) emp_always_assert_msvc_impl(TEST)
   62|       |

   64|       |

   66|  2.70M|    do {                                                                       \

   68|  2.70M|      && EMP_NAMESPACE()::assert_trigger(                                      \
   69|      0|        __FILE__, __LINE__,                                                    \
   70|      0|        EMP_STRINGIFY( EMP_GET_ARG_1(__VA_ARGS__, ~) ),                        \
   71|      0|        EMP_WRAP_ARGS(emp_assert_TO_PAIR, __VA_ARGS__)                         \

   74|  2.70M|    } while(0)

    1|       |/**
    2|       | *  @note This file is part of Empirical, https://github.com/devosoft/Empirical
    3|       | *  @copyright Copyright (C) Michigan State University, MIT Software license; see doc/LICENSE.md
    4|       | *  @date 2016-2020.
    5|       | *
    6|       | *  @file assert.hpp
    7|       | *  @brief A more dynamic replacement for standard library asserts.
    8|       | *  @note Status: RELEASE
    9|       | *
   10|       | *  A replacement for the system-level assert.h, called "emp_assert"
   11|       | *  Added functionality:
   12|       | *   - If compiled with Emscripten, will provide pop-up alerts in a web browser.
   13|       | *   - emp_assert can take additional arguments.  If the assert is triggered, those extra
   14|       | *     arguments will be evaluated and printed.
   15|       | *   - if NDEBUG -or- EMP_NDEBUG is defined, the expression in emp_assert() is not evaluated.
   16|       | *   - if EMP_TDEBUG is defined, emp_assert() goes into test mode and records failures, but
   17|       | *     does not abort.  (useful for unit tests of asserts)
   18|       | *
   19|       | *  Example:
   20|       | *
   21|       | *     int a = 6;
   22|       | *     emp_assert(a==5, a);
   23|       | *
   24|       | *  When compiled in debug mode (i.e. without the -DNDEBUG flag), this will
   25|       | *  trigger an assertion error and print the value of a.
   26|       | */
   27|       |
   28|       |#ifndef EMP_ASSERT_HPP
   29|       |#define EMP_ASSERT_HPP
   30|       |
   31|       |#include "always_assert.hpp"
   32|       |
   33|       |/// NDEBUG hould trigger its EMP equivalent.
   34|       |#ifdef NDEBUG
   35|       |#define EMP_NDEBUG
   36|       |#endif
   37|       |
   38|       |#if defined( EMP_NDEBUG )
   39|       |  /// Ideally, this assert should use the expression (to prevent compiler
   40|       |  /// error), but should not generate any assembly code.
   41|       |  /// For now, just make it blank (other options commented out).
   42|       |  #define emp_assert(...)
   43|       |  // #define emp_assert(EXPR) ((void) sizeof(EXPR) )
   44|       |  // #define emp_assert(EXPR, ...) { constexpr bool __emp_assert_tmp = false && (EXPR); (void) __emp_assert_tmp; }
   45|       |
   46|       |#else
   47|       |  /// Require a specified condition to be true. If it is false, immediately
   48|       |  /// halt execution. Print also extra information on any variables or
   49|       |  /// expressions provided as variadic args. Note: If NDEBUG is defined,
   50|       |  /// emp_assert() will not do anything. Due to macro parsing limitations, extra
   51|       |  /// information will not be printed when compiling with MSVC.
   52|  2.70M|  #define emp_assert(...) emp_always_assert(__VA_ARGS__)
   53|       |

    1|       |/**
    2|       | *  @note This file is part of Empirical, https://github.com/devosoft/Empirical
    3|       | *  @copyright Copyright (C) Michigan State University, MIT Software license; see doc/LICENSE.md
    4|       | *  @date 2016-2018
    5|       | *
    6|       | *  @file macro_math.hpp
    7|       | *  @brief Macros to build a pre-processor calculator system.
    8|       | *  @note Status: RELEASE
    9|       | *
   10|       | *  Working macros include:
   11|       | *   EMP_INC(A)    : converts to result of A+1
   12|       | *   EMP_DEC(A)    : converts to result of A-1
   13|       | *   EMP_SHIFTL(A) : converts to result of A*2
   14|       | *   EMP_SHIFTR(A) : converts to result of A/2
   15|       | *
   16|       | *   EMP_ADD(A,B)  : converts to result of A+B
   17|       | *   EMP_SUB(A,B)  : converts to result of A-B
   18|       | *   EMP_MULT(A,B) : converts to result of A*B
   19|       | *   EMP_DIV(A,B)  : converts to result of A/B
   20|       | *   EMP_MOD(A,B)  : converts to result of A%B
   21|       | *   EMP_LOG2(A)   : converts to (int) log2(A)
   22|       | *
   23|       | *   EMP_NOT(X)     : Logical NOT
   24|       | *   EMP_AND(X, Y)  : Logical AND
   25|       | *   EMP_OR(X, Y)   : Logical OR
   26|       | *   EMP_NAND(X, Y) : Logical NAND
   27|       | *   EMP_NOR(X, Y)  : Logical NOR
   28|       | *   EMP_XOR(X, Y)  : Logical XOR
   29|       | *
   30|       | *   EMP_COUNT_ONES(A) : count the number of ones in the binary representation of A
   31|       | *
   32|       | *   EMP_IF(TEST, T, F) examines the first argument; if it's 0, it resolves to the third
   33|       | *   argument, otherwise it resolves to the second argument.
   34|       | *
   35|       | *  The core idea behind these macros is that we can use brute-force to convert numbers
   36|       | *  to binary, but once there we can easily perform math on them, or convert them to
   37|       | *  yet other forms.
   38|       | *
   39|       | *  Representations include:
   40|       | *   DEC - Standard decimal values (e.g., 91)
   41|       | *   BIN - Binary numbers, with bits separated by commas (e.g.  0, 0, 0,  1, 0,  1, 1, 0, 1, 1 )
   42|       | *   SUM - Like BIN, but stored as zero or magnitude.    (e.g., 0, 0, 0, 64, 0, 16, 8, 0, 2, 1 )
   43|       | *   PACK - Like SUM, but without zeros and in parens    (e.g., (64,16,8,2,1) )
   44|       | *   HEX - Hexidecimal representation (e.g., 0x5B)  [todo]
   45|       | */
   46|       |

    8|       | *  @note Status: BETA
    9|       | *
   10|       | *  This class is a drop-in wrapper for std::vector, adding on bounds checking, both for the
   11|       | *  indexing operator and for the use of iterators (ensure that iterators do not outlive the
   12|       | *  version of vector for which it was created.)
   13|       | *  If EMP_NDEBUG is set then it reverts back to std::vector.
   14|       | *
   15|       | *  @todo Debug code: member functions that take iterators should also take emp iterators that verify
   16|       | *        whether those iterators are valid.
   17|       | */
   18|       |

  331|       |  /// Build a specialized debug wrapper for emp::vector<bool>
  332|       |  template <typename... Ts>
  333|       |  class vector<bool, Ts...> : public std::vector<bool,Ts...> {
  334|       |  private:
  335|       |    using this_t = emp::vector<bool,Ts...>;
  336|       |    using stdv_t = std::vector<bool,Ts...>;
  337|       |
  338|       |    /// Setup a threshold; if we try to make a vector bigger than MAX_SIZE, throw a warning.
  339|       |    constexpr static const size_t MAX_SIZE = 2000000001;
  340|       |
  341|       |  public:
  342|       |    using iterator = typename stdv_t::iterator;
  343|       |    using const_iterator = typename stdv_t::const_iterator;
  344|       |    using value_type = bool;
  345|       |    using size_type = typename stdv_t::size_type;
  346|       |    using reference = typename stdv_t::reference;
  347|       |    using const_reference = typename stdv_t::const_reference;
  348|       |
  349|       |    vector() : stdv_t() {};
  350|       |    vector(const this_t & _in) : stdv_t(_in) {};
  351|       |    vector(size_t size) : stdv_t(size) { emp_assert(size < MAX_SIZE, size); }
  352|       |    vector(size_t size, bool val) : stdv_t(size, val) { emp_assert(size < MAX_SIZE, size); }
  353|       |    vector(std::initializer_list<bool> in_list) : stdv_t(in_list) { ; }
  354|       |    vector(const stdv_t & in) : stdv_t(in) { ; }         // Emergency fallback conversion.
  355|       |    template <typename InputIt>
  356|       |    vector(InputIt first, InputIt last) : stdv_t(first, last){;}
  357|       |
  358|       |
  359|       |    // operator stdv_t &() { return v; }
  360|       |    // operator const stdv_t &() const { return v; }
  361|       |
  362|       |    void resize(size_t new_size) { emp_assert(new_size < MAX_SIZE, new_size); stdv_t::resize(new_size); }
  363|       |    void resize(size_t new_size, bool val) {
  364|       |      emp_assert(new_size < MAX_SIZE, new_size);
  365|       |      stdv_t::resize(new_size, val);
  366|       |    }
  367|       |    this_t & operator=(const this_t &) = default;
  368|       |

  370|       |      emp_assert(pos < stdv_t::size(), pos, stdv_t::size());
  371|       |      return stdv_t::operator[](pos);
  372|       |    }
  373|       |

   18|       |  // The MapProxy class is returned in the place of a mapped type to track usage.
   19|       |  // @CAO: Note that ideally we should have two proxy classes; one for when the wrapped type is a
   20|       |  //       class itself and we can derive from it (thus, perhaps, keeping member functions working?)
   21|       |  //       and the other being when it is a base type (and there are no member functions).
   22|       |  //       Fundamentally this problem comes from the fact that we cannot overload the . operator,
   23|       |  //       and thus cannot get it working directly for the Proxy class.
   24|       |  template <typename T>
   25|       |  class MapProxy {
   26|       |  private:
   27|       |    T & value;
   28|       |    bool is_init;  ///< Has this value been set yet?
   29|       |  public:

   31|       |

   33|       |    const T & emp_GetValue() const { emp_assert(is_init); return value; }
   34|       |    bool emp_IsInit() const { return is_init; }

    1|       |/**
    2|       | *  @note This file is part of Empirical, https://github.com/devosoft/Empirical
    3|       | *  @copyright Copyright (C) Michigan State University, MIT Software license; see doc/LICENSE.md
    4|       | *  @date 2016-2020.
    5|       | *
    6|       | *  @file Ptr.hpp
    7|       | *  @brief A wrapper for pointers that does careful memory tracking (but only in debug mode).
    8|       | *  @note Status: BETA
    9|       | *
   10|       | *  Ptr objects behave as normal pointers under most conditions.  However, if a program is
   11|       | *  compiled with EMP_TRACK_MEM set, then these pointers perform extra tests to ensure that
   12|       | *  they point to valid memory and that memory is freed before pointers are released.
   13|       | *
   14|       | *  If you trip an assert, you can re-do the run a track a specific pointer by defining
   15|       | *  EMP_ABORT_PTR_NEW or EMP_ABORT_PTR_DELETE to the ID of the pointer in question.  This will
   16|       | *  allow you to track the pointer more easily in a debugger.
   17|       | *
   18|       | *  @todo Track information about emp::vector and emp::array objects to make sure we don't
   19|       | *    point directly into them? (A resize() could make such pointers invalid!) Or better, warn
   20|       | *    it vector memory could have moved.
   21|       | *  @todo Get working with threads
   22|       | */
   23|       |

  693|       |    /// Does this Ptr point to different memory positions as a raw pointer?
  694|       |    bool operator!=(const TYPE * in_ptr) const { return ptr != in_ptr; }
  695|       |
  696|       |    /// Does this Ptr point to a memory position before a raw pointer?
  697|       |    bool operator<(const TYPE * in_ptr)  const { return ptr < in_ptr; }
  698|       |
  699|       |    /// Does this Ptr point to a memory position before or equal to a raw pointer?
  700|       |    bool operator<=(const TYPE * in_ptr) const { return ptr <= in_ptr; }
  701|       |
  702|       |    /// Does this Ptr point to a memory position after a raw pointer?
  703|       |    bool operator>(const TYPE * in_ptr)  const { return ptr > in_ptr; }
  704|       |
  705|       |    /// Does this Ptr point to a memory position after or equal to a raw pointer?
  706|       |    bool operator>=(const TYPE * in_ptr) const { return ptr >= in_ptr; }
  707|       |
  708|       |
  709|       |    /// Some debug testing functions
  710|       |    int DebugGetCount() const { return Tracker().GetIDCount(id); }
  711|       |    bool DebugIsArray() const { return Tracker().IsArrayID(id); }
  712|       |    size_t DebugGetArrayBytes() const { return Tracker().GetArrayBytes(id); }
  713|       |    bool DebugIsActive() const { return Tracker().IsActiveID(id); }
  714|       |
  715|       |    bool OK() const {
  716|       |      // Untracked ID's should not have pointers in the Tracker.
  717|       |      if (id == UNTRACKED_ID) return !Tracker().HasPtr(ptr);
  718|       |
  719|       |      // Make sure this pointer is linked to the correct info.
  720|       |      if (Tracker().GetInfo(id).GetPtr() != ptr) return false;
  721|       |
  722|       |      // And make sure that info itself is okay.
  723|       |      return Tracker().GetInfo(id).OK();
  724|       |    }

  747|       |
  748|       |  template <typename TYPE>
  749|       |  class BasePtr {
  750|       |  protected:
  751|       |    TYPE * ptr;                 ///< The raw pointer associated with this Ptr object.
  752|       |
  753|       |  public:
  754|       |    BasePtr(TYPE * in_ptr) : ptr(in_ptr) { }
  755|       |
  756|       |    // Dereference a pointer.
  757|       |    [[nodiscard]] TYPE & operator*() const { return *ptr; }
  758|       |
  759|       |    // Follow a pointer.
  760|       |    TYPE * operator->() const { return ptr; }
  761|       |
  762|       |    // Should implement operator->* to follow a pointer to a member function.
  763|       |    // For an example, see:
  764|       |    //  https://stackoverflow.com/questions/27634036/overloading-operator-in-c
  765|       |
  766|       |    // Indexing into array
  767|       |    TYPE & operator[](size_t pos) const { return ptr[pos]; }
  768|       |  };
  769|       |
  770|       |  /// Base class with functionality only needed in void pointers.
  771|       |  template <> class BasePtr<void> {
  772|       |  protected: void * ptr;                 ///< The raw pointer associated with this Ptr object.
  773|       |  public: BasePtr(void * in_ptr) : ptr(in_ptr) { }
  774|       |  };
  775|       |
  776|       |  template <> class BasePtr<const void> {
  777|       |  protected: const void * ptr;           ///< The raw pointer associated with this Ptr object.
  778|       |  public: BasePtr(const void * in_ptr) : ptr(in_ptr) { }
  779|       |  };
  780|       |
  781|       |  template <typename TYPE>
  782|       |  class Ptr : public BasePtr<TYPE> {
  783|       |  private:
  784|       |    using BasePtr<TYPE>::ptr;
  785|       |
  786|       |  public:
  787|       |    using element_type = TYPE;
  788|       |
  789|       |    /// Default constructor
  790|       |    Ptr() : BasePtr<TYPE>(nullptr) {}
  791|       |
  792|       |    /// Copy constructor
  793|       |    Ptr(const Ptr<TYPE> & _in) : BasePtr<TYPE>(_in.ptr) {}
  794|       |
  795|       |    /// Construct from raw ptr
  796|       |    template <typename T2> Ptr(T2 * in_ptr, bool=false) : BasePtr<TYPE>(in_ptr) {}
  797|       |
  798|       |    /// Construct from array
  799|       |    template <typename T2> Ptr(T2 * _ptr, size_t, bool) : BasePtr<TYPE>(_ptr) {}
  800|       |
  801|       |    /// From compatible Ptr
  802|       |    template <typename T2> Ptr(Ptr<T2> _in) : BasePtr<TYPE>(_in.Raw()) {}
  803|       |
  804|       |    /// From nullptr
  805|       |    Ptr(std::nullptr_t) : Ptr() {}
  806|       |
  807|       |    /// Destructor
  808|       |    ~Ptr() { ; }
  809|       |
  810|       |    [[nodiscard]] bool IsNull() const { return ptr == nullptr; }
  811|       |    [[nodiscard]] TYPE * Raw() const { return ptr; }
  812|       |    [[nodiscard]] TYPE * Raw(size_t pos) const { return &(ptr[pos]); }
  813|       |    template <typename T2> Ptr<T2> Cast() const { return (T2*) ptr; }
  814|       |    template <typename T2> Ptr<T2> ConstCast() const { return const_cast<T2*>(ptr); }
  815|       |    template <typename T2> Ptr<T2> DynamicCast() const { return dynamic_cast<T2*>(ptr); }
  816|       |    template <typename T2> Ptr<T2> ReinterpretCast() const { return reinterpret_cast<T2*>(ptr); }
  817|       |
  818|       |    template <typename... T>
  819|       |    void New(T &&... args) { ptr = new TYPE(std::forward<T>(args)...); }  // New raw pointer.
  820|       |    void NewArray(size_t array_size) { ptr = new TYPE[array_size]; }
  821|       |    void Delete() { delete ptr; }
  822|       |    void DeleteArray() { delete [] ptr; }
  823|       |
  824|       |    size_t Hash() const {
  825|       |      static constexpr size_t shift = internal::Log2(1 + sizeof(TYPE));  // Chop off useless bits...
  826|       |      return (size_t)(ptr) >> shift;
  827|       |    }
  828|       |    struct hash_t { size_t operator()(const Ptr<TYPE> & t) const { return t.Hash(); } };
  829|       |
  830|       |    // Copy assignments
  831|       |    Ptr<TYPE> & operator=(const Ptr<TYPE> & _in) { ptr = _in.ptr; return *this; }
  832|       |
  833|       |    // Assign to compatible Ptr or raw (non-managed) pointer.
  834|       |    template <typename T2> Ptr<TYPE> & operator=(T2 * _in) { ptr = _in; return *this; }
  835|       |    template <typename T2> Ptr<TYPE> & operator=(Ptr<T2> _in) { ptr = _in.Raw(); return *this; }
  836|       |
  837|       |    // Auto-cast to raw pointer type.
  838|       |    operator TYPE *() { return ptr; }
  839|       |
  840|       |    operator bool() { return ptr != nullptr; }
  841|       |    operator bool() const { return ptr != nullptr; }
  842|       |
  843|       |    // Comparisons to other Ptr objects
  844|       |    bool operator==(const Ptr<TYPE> & in_ptr) const { return ptr == in_ptr.ptr; }
  845|       |    bool operator!=(const Ptr<TYPE> & in_ptr) const { return ptr != in_ptr.ptr; }
  846|       |    bool operator<(const Ptr<TYPE> & in_ptr)  const { return ptr < in_ptr.ptr; }
  847|       |    bool operator<=(const Ptr<TYPE> & in_ptr) const { return ptr <= in_ptr.ptr; }
  848|       |    bool operator>(const Ptr<TYPE> & in_ptr)  const { return ptr > in_ptr.ptr; }
  849|       |    bool operator>=(const Ptr<TYPE> & in_ptr) const { return ptr >= in_ptr.ptr; }
  850|       |
  851|       |    // Comparisons to raw pointers.
  852|       |    bool operator==(const TYPE * in_ptr) const { return ptr == in_ptr; }
  853|       |    bool operator!=(const TYPE * in_ptr) const { return ptr != in_ptr; }
  854|       |    bool operator<(const TYPE * in_ptr)  const { return ptr < in_ptr; }
  855|       |    bool operator<=(const TYPE * in_ptr) const { return ptr <= in_ptr; }
  856|       |    bool operator>(const TYPE * in_ptr)  const { return ptr > in_ptr; }
  857|       |    bool operator>=(const TYPE * in_ptr) const { return ptr >= in_ptr; }
  858|       |
  859|       |    // Stubs for debug-related functions when outside debug mode.
  860|       |    int DebugGetCount() const { return -1; }
  861|       |    bool DebugIsArray() const { emp_assert(false); return false; }
  862|       |    size_t DebugGetArrayBytes() const { return 0; }
  863|       |    bool DebugIsActive() const { return true; }

  865|       |  };

  894|       |  /// Create a new Ptr of the target type; use the args in the constructor.

  896|       |  [[nodiscard]] Ptr<T> NewPtr(ARGS &&... args) {
  897|       |    auto ptr = new T(std::forward<ARGS>(args)...);
  898|       |    // auto ptr = (T*) malloc (sizeof(T));         // Build a new raw pointer.
  899|       |    // emp_assert(ptr);                            // No exceptions in emscripten; assert alloc!
  900|       |    // new (ptr) T(std::forward<ARGS>(args)...);   // Special new that uses allocated space.

  902|       |  }

  911|       |    emp::vector<Ptr<T>> out_ptrs(in.size());

  915|       |

  919|       |    emp::vector<Ptr<T>> out_ptrs(in.size());

  923|       |

    1|       |/**
    2|       | *  @note This file is part of Empirical, https://github.com/devosoft/Empirical
    3|       | *  @copyright Copyright (C) Michigan State University, MIT Software license; see doc/LICENSE.md
    4|       | *  @date 2016-2018
    5|       | *
    6|       | *  @file  errors.hpp
    7|       | *  @brief Tools to help manage various problems in command-line or Emscripten-based applications.
    8|       | *  @note Status: ALPHA
    9|       | *
   10|       | *  There are three possible recipiants for all errors/warnings.
   11|       | *  - The end-user if the problem stems from inputs they provided to the executable.
   12|       | *  - The library user if the problem is due to mis-use of library functionality.
   13|       | *  - The library developers if something that should be impossible occurs.
   14|       | *
   15|       | *  There are also three types of problmes to notify about:
   16|       | *  - Warnings if something looks suspicious, but isn't technically a problem.
   17|       | *  - Errors if something has gone so horribly wrong that it is impossible to recover from.
   18|       | *  - Exceptions if something didn't go the way we expected, but we can still recover.
   19|       | *
   20|       | *  In general, most of the content of this file is targeted at providing useful tools for library
   21|       | *  users; end-users should receive more customized messages and asserts should capture
   22|       | *  suposedly "impossible" situations that none-the-less occur in the library itself.
   23|       | *
   24|       | *  NOTES:
   25|       | *  - Whenever possible, exceptions should be preferred.  They are more specific than warnings,
   26|       | *    but don't halt execution like errors.
   27|       | *  - Warnings should always detail what should be done differently to surpress the warning.
   28|       | *
   29|       | *
   30|       | *  @todo We should move over to a pure replacement for exceptions.
   31|       | *    - Different types of exceptions can trigger a signal.  Actions should return a bool
   32|       | *      indicating whether the exception was fixed.
   33|       | *    - Remaining exceptions are recorded and passed back up the chain to (hopefully) be caught.
   34|       | *    - Uncaught exceptions should have a default behavior when Resolved.  Exceptions could have
   35|       | *      various resolve times: Next exception added, Next exception check, when ResolveExceptions()
   36|       | *      is run, End of program, or ASAP. (perhaps)
   37|       |*/
   38|       |

   60|       |  /// Return the position of the first one bit
   61|       |  template <typename T>
   62|       |  inline constexpr size_t find_bit(T val) { return count_bits( (~val) & (val-1) ); }
   63|       |
   64|       |  /// Return the position of the first one bit AND REMOVE IT.
   65|       |  template <typename T>
   66|       |  inline size_t pop_bit(T & val) {
   67|       |    const size_t pos = find_bit(val);
   68|       |    val &= ~(1 << pos);
   69|       |    return pos;
   70|       |  }
   71|       |
   72|       |  /// A compile-time bit counter.
   73|       |  template <typename TYPE>
   74|       |  static constexpr int CountOnes(TYPE x) { return x == 0 ? 0 : (CountOnes(x/2) + (x&1)); }
   75|       |
   76|       |  /// Quick bit-mask generator for low bits.
   77|       |  template <typename TYPE>
   78|       |  static constexpr TYPE MaskLow(std::size_t num_bits) {
   79|       |    return (num_bits == 8*sizeof(TYPE)) ? ((TYPE)-1) : ((((TYPE)1) << num_bits) - 1);
   80|       |  }
   81|       |
   82|       |  /// Quick bit-mask generator for high bits.
   83|       |  template <typename TYPE>
   84|       |  static constexpr TYPE MaskHigh(std::size_t num_bits) {
   85|       |    return MaskLow<TYPE>(num_bits) << (8*sizeof(TYPE)-num_bits);
   86|       |  }

   91|      0|    TYPE last = 0;

   94|      0|      val |= (val >> shift);  // Copy 1's over to the shifted position of val.
   95|      0|      shift <<= 1;            // Double the size of the shift for the next loop.
   96|      0|      if (shift == 0) return 0;
   97|      0|    }
   98|      0|    return val;

    6|       |//  The ConfigManager templated class handles the building and configuration of new objects
    7|       |//  of the target type.
    8|       |//
    9|       |//  The manager is created with two keywords; one for the type of the managed class, and the
   10|       |//  other for the keyword to trigger commands for it.
   11|       |//
   12|       |//  For example, if we're configuring an instruction set, the type might be 'inst_set' and the
   13|       |//  keyword might be 'inst'.  Then the configuration file can have lines like:
   14|       |//
   15|       |//     new inst_lib 4stack
   16|       |//     inst nopA
   17|       |//     inst inc
   18|       |//     inst divide cycle_cost=10
   19|       |//     ...
   20|       |
   21|       |
   22|       |#ifndef EMP_CONFIG_MANAGER_H
   23|       |#define EMP_CONFIG_MANAGER_H
   24|       |
   25|       |#include <functional>
   26|       |#include <iostream>
   27|       |#include <map>
   28|       |#include <string>
   29|       |#include <sstream>
   30|       |
   31|       |#include "../base/errors.hpp"
   32|       |#include "../tools/string_utils.hpp"
   33|       |
   34|       |namespace emp {
   35|       |
   36|       |  class ConfigManager_Base {
   37|       |  protected:
   38|       |    const std::string type_keyword;
   39|       |    const std::string command_keyword;
   40|       |
   41|       |  public:
   42|       |    ConfigManager_Base(const std::string & _type, const std::string & _command)
   43|      0|      : type_keyword(_type), command_keyword(_command) { ; }
   44|      0|    virtual ~ConfigManager_Base() { ; }
   45|       |
   46|      0|    const std::string & GetTypeKeyword() const { return type_keyword; }
   47|      0|    const std::string & GetCommandKeyword() const { return command_keyword; }
   48|       |
   49|       |    virtual void NewObject(const std::string & obj_name) = 0;
   50|       |    virtual void UseObject(const std::string & obj_name) = 0;
   51|       |    virtual bool CommandCallback(const std::string & command) = 0;
   52|       |  };
   53|       |
   54|       |  template <class MANAGED_TYPE> class ConfigManager : public ConfigManager_Base {
   55|       |  private:
   56|       |    std::map<std::string, MANAGED_TYPE *> name_map;
   57|       |    MANAGED_TYPE * cur_obj;
   58|       |    std::function<bool(MANAGED_TYPE &, std::string)> callback_fun;
   59|       |
   60|       |  public:
   61|       |    ConfigManager(const std::string & _type, const std::string & _command,
   62|       |                  std::function<bool(MANAGED_TYPE &, std::string)> _fun)
   63|       |      : ConfigManager_Base(_type, _command)
   64|       |      , cur_obj(nullptr)
   65|       |      , callback_fun(_fun)
   66|       |    {
   67|       |    }
   68|       |    ~ConfigManager() { ; }
   69|       |
   70|       |    bool HasObject(const std::string & obj_name) {
   71|       |      return (name_map.find(obj_name) != name_map.end());
   72|       |    }
   73|       |
   74|       |    void NewObject(const std::string & obj_name) {
   75|       |      if (HasObject(obj_name) == true) {
   76|       |        std::stringstream ss;
   77|       |        ss << "Building new object of type '" << type_keyword << "' named '"
   78|       |           << obj_name << "' when one already exists. Replacing." << std::endl;
   79|       |        NotifyError(ss.str());
   80|       |        delete name_map[obj_name];
   81|       |      }
   82|       |      cur_obj = new MANAGED_TYPE;
   83|       |      name_map[obj_name] = cur_obj;
   84|       |    }
   85|       |
   86|       |    void UseObject(const std::string & obj_name) {
   87|       |      if (HasObject(obj_name) == false) {
   88|       |        std::stringstream ss;
   89|       |        ss << "Trying to use object of type '" << type_keyword << "' named '"
   90|       |           << obj_name << "', but does not exist. Ignoring." << std::endl;
   91|       |        NotifyError(ss.str());
   92|       |        return;
   93|       |      }
   94|       |      cur_obj = name_map[obj_name];
   95|       |    }
   96|       |
   97|       |    bool CommandCallback(const std::string & command) {
   98|       |      if (cur_obj == nullptr) {
   99|       |        std::stringstream ss;
  100|       |        ss << "Must build new '" << type_keyword << "' object before using command '"
  101|       |           << command_keyword << "'.  Ignoring." << std::endl;
  102|       |        NotifyError(ss.str());
  103|       |        return false;
  104|       |      }
  105|       |
  106|       |      return callback_fun(*cur_obj, command);
  107|       |    }
  108|       |  };
  109|       |
  110|       |}
  111|       |
  112|       |#endif

    1|       |/**
    2|       | *  @note This file is part of Empirical, https://github.com/devosoft/Empirical
    3|       | *  @copyright Copyright (C) Michigan State University, MIT Software license; see doc/LICENSE.md
    4|       | *  @date 2016-2019.
    5|       | *
    6|       | *  @file  config.hpp
    7|       | *  @brief Maintains a set of configuration options.
    8|       | *
    9|       | *  This file defines a master configuration option Config, whose values can be loaded
   10|       | *  at runtime or else set as constant values throughout the code.
   11|       | *
   12|       | *  Assuming you have an emp::Config object called config, you can:
   13|       | *
   14|       | *  access a setting value:            config.SETTING_NAME()
   15|       | *  adjust a setting value:            config.SETTING_NAME(new_value)
   16|       | *  determine if a setting is locked:  config.SETTING_NAME_is_const()
   17|       | *  lookup a setting dynamically:      config("SETTING_NAME")
   18|       | *  adjust a setting dynamically:      config("SETTING_NAME", "new_value")
   19|       | *
   20|       | *  load settings from a stream:       config.Read(stream);
   21|       | *  load settings from a file:         config.Read(filename);
   22|       | *  save settings to a stream:         config.Write(stream);
   23|       | *  save settings to a file:           config.Write(filename);
   24|       | *
   25|       | *  write settings macros to a stream: config.WriteMacros(stream);
   26|       | *  write settings macros to a file:   config.WriteMacros(filename);
   27|       | *
   28|       | *
   29|       | *  The configuration files generated can use the following keywords in order to
   30|       | *  configure this object:
   31|       | *   include OTHER_FILENAME         -- Load in all data from another file.
   32|       | *   set SETTING_NAME VALUE         -- Set a basic configuration setting.
   33|       | *   new OBJECT_TYPE OBJECT_NAME    -- Create a new config object of a managed class.
   34|       | *   use OBJECT_TYPE OBJECT_NAME    -- Use a previouly created configuration object.
   35|       | */
   36|       |

  301|       |    // Process a line by:
  302|       |    // * Remove excess whitespace
  303|       |    // * Expand all variables beginning with a $ in config line.
  304|       |    // * If wrap-around, move line to extras
  305|      0|    void ProcessLine(std::string & cur_line, std::string & extras) {

  307|      0|      if (extras.size()) cur_line.insert(0, extras);
  308|      0|      extras.resize(0);

  310|      0|

  314|      0|        if (cur_char == '\\') {
  315|      0|          if (pos+1 == cur_line.size()) {                    // If backslash is at end of line...

  329|      0|          }

  332|      0|        else if (cur_char == '#') {
  333|      0|          cur_line.resize(pos);
  334|      0|        }

  353|      0|      }

  581|       |    /// Read in from a text representation (typically a file) to set the state of Config.
  582|       |    /// Return success state.
  583|      0|    bool Read(std::istream & input, const std::string & cur_namespace="") {
  584|      0|      // Load in the file one line at a time and process each line.
  585|      0|      std::string cur_line, extras;
  586|      0|      delay_warnings++;

  591|      0|        ProcessLine(cur_line, extras);         // Clean up line; act on aliases.
  592|      0|
  593|      0|        if (cur_line == "") continue;          // Skip empty lines.
  594|      0|
  595|      0|        std::string command = emp::string_pop_word(cur_line);
  596|      0|
  597|      0|        if (command == "set") {
  598|      0|          // Set a specific value.
  599|      0|          std::string setting_name = emp::string_pop_word(cur_line);
  600|      0|          Set(setting_name, cur_line);
  601|      0|        }
  602|      0|        else if (command == "include") {
  603|      0|          // Determine the filename to include.
  604|      0|          std::string filename = emp::string_pop_word(cur_line);
  605|      0|
  606|      0|          // Process the new file (before automatically coming back to this one)
  607|      0|          Read(filename);
  608|      0|        }
  609|      0|        else if (command == "namespace") {
  610|      0|          std::string namespace_name = emp::string_pop_word(cur_line);
  611|      0|          if (cur_line.size() > 0) {
  612|      0|            warnings << "namespace " << namespace_name
  613|      0|                     << " cannot have additional arguments.  Ignoring.\n";
  614|      0|          }
  615|      0|          if (emp::Has(namespace_map, namespace_name) == false) {
  616|      0|            emp::NotifyError(emp::to_string("Unknown namespace '", namespace_name, "'.  Aborting."));
  617|      0|            return false;
  618|      0|          }
  619|      0|          Config * ns_config = namespace_map[namespace_name];
  620|      0|          ns_config->Read(input, namespace_name);
  621|      0|        }
  622|      0|        else if (command == "end_namespace") {
  623|      0|          std::string namespace_name = emp::string_pop_word(cur_line);
  624|      0|          if (cur_line.size() > 0) {

  630|      0|                                            "' while in namespace '", cur_namespace, "'.  Aborting."));
  631|      0|            return false;

  633|      0|          return true;
  634|      0|        }

  651|      0|          // Run this custom command.

  666|      0|      }

   30|       |  /// Keep track of everything associated with periodically printing data to a file.
   31|       |  /// Maintain a set of functions for calculating the desired measurements at each point in
   32|       |  /// time that they are required. It also handles the formating of the file.
   33|       |
   34|       |  class DataFile {
   35|       |  protected:
   36|       |    using fun_t = void(std::ostream &);
   37|       |    using time_fun_t = std::function<bool(size_t)>;
   38|       |    using pre_fun_t = std::function<void()>;
   39|       |
   40|       |    std::string filename;            ///< Name of the file that we are printing to (if one exists)
   41|       |    std::ostream * os;               ///< Stream to print to.
   42|       |    FunctionSet<fun_t> funs;         ///< Set of functions to call, one per column in the file.
   43|       |    FunctionSet<void()> pre_funs;    ///< Set of functions to call before calculating data.

   46|       |    time_fun_t timing_fun;           ///< Function to determine updates to print on (default: all)
   47|       |

   49|       |    std::string line_spacer;         ///< What should we print between entries?

  171|       |    /// want to call one of the more specific ones.
  172|       |    /// @param fun function to call to get the value to print

  419|       |
  420|       |    /// Add multiple functions that pull common stats measurements (mean, variance, min, and max)
  421|       |    /// from the DataNode \c node.
  422|       |    /// Requires that \c node have the \c data::Stats or \c data::FullStats modifier.
  423|       |    /// \c key and \c desc will have the name of the stat appended to the beginning.
  424|       |    /// Note: excludes standard deviation, because it is easily calculated from variance
  425|       |    /// Note: Setting \c pull and/or \c reset to true only pulls on first statistic
  426|       |    /// calculated and only resets on the last. Otherwise there would be a risk of data loss or
  427|       |    /// at least massive replication of computational effort. Even still, think carefully
  428|       |    /// before setting either of these to true when you're drawing varied information from the
  429|       |    /// same node.
  430|       |    /// @param node DataNode to get data from
  431|       |    /// @param key Keyword associated with this column (gets used as a column name for this data)
  432|       |    /// @param desc Full description of this data (used by \c PrintHeaderComment)
  433|       |    /// @param reset If this parameter is set true, we will call Reset on that DataNode after pulling the
  434|       |    /// current value from the node
  435|       |    /// @param pull Should the node pull data before this statistic is calculated?
  436|       |    template <typename VAL_TYPE, emp::data... MODS>
  437|       |    void AddStats(DataNode<VAL_TYPE, MODS...> & node, const std::string & key="", const std::string & desc="", const bool & reset=false, const bool & pull=false) {
  438|       |      AddMean(node, "mean_" + key, "mean of " + desc, false, pull);
  439|       |      AddMin(node, "min_" + key, "min of " + desc);
  440|       |      AddMax(node, "max_" + key, "max of " + desc);
  441|       |      AddVariance(node, "variance_" + key, "variance of " + desc, reset);
  442|       |    }
  443|       |
  444|       |
  445|       |    /// Add multiple functions that pull all stats measurements (mean, variance, min, max,
  446|       |    /// skew, and kurtosis) from the DataNode \c node
  447|       |    /// Requires that \c node have the \c data::Stats or \c data::FullStats modifier.
  448|       |    /// \c key and \c desc will have the name of the stat appended to the beginning.
  449|       |    /// Note: excludes standard deviation, because it is easily calculated from variance
  450|       |    /// Note: Setting \c pull and/or \c reset to true only pulls on first statistic
  451|       |    /// calculated and only resets on the last. Otherwise there would be a risk of data loss or
  452|       |    /// at least massive replication of computational effort. Even still, think carefully
  453|       |    /// before setting either of these to true when you're drawing varied information from the
  454|       |    /// same node.
  455|       |    /// @param node DataNode to get data from
  456|       |    /// @param key Keyword associated with this column (gets used as a column name for this data)
  457|       |    /// @param desc Full description of this data (used by \c PrintHeaderComment)
  458|       |    /// @param reset If this parameter is set true, we will call Reset on that DataNode after pulling the
  459|       |    /// current value from the node
  460|       |    /// @param pull Should the node pull data before this statistic is calculated?
  461|       |    template <typename VAL_TYPE, emp::data... MODS>
  462|       |    void AddAllStats(DataNode<VAL_TYPE, MODS...> & node, const std::string & key="", const std::string & desc="", const bool & reset=false, const bool & pull=false) {
  463|       |      AddStats(node, key, desc, false, pull);
  464|       |      AddSkew(node, "skew_" + key, "skew of " + desc);
  465|       |      AddKurtosis(node, "kurtosis_" + key, "kurtosis of " + desc, reset);
  466|       |    }
  467|       |
  468|       |    /// Add a function that always pulls the count of the \c bin_id 'th bin of the histogram
  469|       |    /// from \c node. Requires that \c node have the \c data::Histogram modifier and at least
  470|       |    /// \c bin_id bins.
  471|       |    /// @param node DataNode to get data from
  472|       |    /// @param bin_id id of bin to log data from (0-indexed)
  473|       |    /// @param key Keyword associated with this column (gets used as a column name for this data)
  474|       |    /// @param desc Full description of this data (used by \c PrintHeaderComment)
  475|       |    /// @param reset If this parameter is set true, we will call Reset on that DataNode after pulling the
  476|       |    /// current value from the node
  477|       |    /// @param pull Should the node pull data before this statistic is calculated?
  478|       |    template <typename VAL_TYPE, emp::data... MODS>
  479|       |    size_t AddHistBin(DataNode<VAL_TYPE, MODS...> & node, size_t bin_id, const std::string & key="", const std::string & desc="", const bool & reset=false, const bool & pull=false) {
  480|       |      std::function<fun_t> in_fun =
  481|       |        [&node,bin_id,reset, pull](std::ostream & os){
  482|       |          if (pull) node.PullData();
  483|       |          os << node.GetHistCount(bin_id);
  484|       |          if (reset) node.Reset();
  485|       |        };
  486|       |      return Add(in_fun, key, desc);
  487|       |    }
  488|       |
  489|       |    /// Add a set of functions that always pull the count of each bin of the histogram
  490|       |    /// from \c node. Requires that \c node have the \c data::Histogram modifier.
  491|       |    /// Note: Setting \c pull and/or \c reset to true only pulls on first statistic
  492|       |    /// calculated and only resets on the last. Otherwise there would be a risk of data loss or
  493|       |    /// at least massive replication of computational effort. Even still, think carefully
  494|       |    /// before setting either of these to true when you're drawing varied information from the
  495|       |    /// same node.
  496|       |    /// @param node DataNode to get data from
  497|       |    /// @param key Keyword associated with this column (gets used as a column name for this data)
  498|       |    /// @param desc Full description of this data (used by \c PrintHeaderComment)
  499|       |    /// @param reset If this parameter is set true, we will call Reset on that DataNode after pulling the
  500|       |    /// current value from the node
  501|       |    /// @param pull Should the node pull data before this statistic is calculated?
  502|       |    template <typename VAL_TYPE, emp::data... MODS>
  503|       |    void AddAllHistBins(DataNode<VAL_TYPE, MODS...> & node, const std::string & key="", const std::string & desc="", const bool & reset=false, const bool & pull=false) {
  504|       |        bool actual_reset = false;
  505|       |        bool actual_pull = pull;
  506|       |        for (size_t i = 0; i < node.GetHistCounts().size(); i++) {
  507|       |          if (i == node.GetHistCounts().size() - 1) {
  508|       |            actual_reset = reset;
  509|       |          }
  510|       |          AddHistBin(node, i, key + "_bin_" + emp::to_string(i), desc + " bin " + emp::to_string(i), actual_reset, actual_pull);
  511|       |          actual_pull = false;
  512|       |        }
  513|       |    }
  514|       |
  515|       |    /// Add a function that always pulls the inferiority (mean divided by max) from the specified DataNode.
  516|       |    /// Requires that node have the data::Range or data::FullRange modifier.
  517|       |    /// @param node DataNode to get data from
  518|       |    /// @param key Keyword associated with this column (gets used as a column name for this data)
  519|       |    /// @param desc Full description of this data (used by \c PrintHeaderComment)
  520|       |    /// @param reset If this parameter is set true, we will call Reset on that DataNode after pulling the
  521|       |    /// current value from the node
  522|       |    /// @param pull Should the node pull data before this statistic is calculated?
  523|       |    template <typename VAL_TYPE, emp::data... MODS>
  524|       |    size_t AddInferiority(DataNode<VAL_TYPE, MODS...> & node, const std::string & key="", const std::string & desc="", const bool & reset=false, const bool & pull=false) {
  525|       |      std::function<fun_t> in_fun = [&node, reset, pull](std::ostream & os){
  526|       |        if (pull) node.PullData();
  527|       |        VAL_TYPE inf = (node.GetMax() == 0) ? 0 : (node.GetMean() / node.GetMax());
  528|       |        os << inf;
  529|       |        if (reset) node.Reset();
  530|       |      };
  531|       |      return Add(in_fun, key, desc);
  532|       |    }
  533|       |  };
  534|       |
  535|       |
  536|       |  template <typename container_t>
  537|       |  class ContainerDataFile;
  538|       |
  539|       |  // This handles all possible forms of pointers to containers.
  540|       |  #ifndef DOXYGEN_SHOULD_SKIP_THIS
  541|       |  namespace internal {
  542|       |
  543|       |    template <typename container_t>
  544|       |    struct update_impl {
  545|       |      void Update(ContainerDataFile<container_t> * df) {
  546|       |        using data_t = typename container_t::value_type;
  547|       |
  548|       |        for (const data_t & d : df->GetCurrentRows()) {
  549|       |          df->FilterThenOutputLine(d);
  550|       |        }
  551|       |      }
  552|       |    };
  553|       |
  554|       |    template <typename container_t>
  555|       |    struct update_impl<Ptr<container_t>> {
  556|       |      void Update(ContainerDataFile<Ptr<container_t>> * df) {
  557|       |        using data_t = typename remove_ptr_type<container_t>::type::value_type;
  558|       |
  559|       |        for (const data_t & d : *(df->GetCurrentRows())) {
  560|       |          df->FilterThenOutputLine(d);
  561|       |        }
  562|       |      }
  563|       |    };
  564|       |
  565|       |    template <typename container_t>
  566|       |    struct update_impl<container_t*> {
  567|       |      void Update(ContainerDataFile<container_t*> * df) {
  568|       |        using data_t = typename remove_ptr_type<container_t>::type::value_type;
  569|       |
  570|       |        for (const data_t & d : *(df->GetCurrentRows())) {
  571|       |          df->FilterThenOutputLine(d);
  572|       |        }
  573|       |      }
  574|       |    };
  575|       |
  576|       |  }
  577|       |  #endif // DOXYGEN_SHOULD_SKIP_THIS
  578|       |
  579|       |  /// Sometimes you may want a data file where a set
  580|       |  /// of functions is run on every item in a container
  581|       |  /// every time you write to the file. ContainerDataFiles do that.
  582|       |  ///
  583|       |  /// Note: CONTAINER type can be a pointer to a container and the
  584|       |  /// datafile will handle dereferencing it appropriately.
  585|       |
  586|       |  template <typename CONTAINER>
  587|       |  class ContainerDataFile : public DataFile {
  588|       |    protected:
  589|       |    // The container type cannot be a reference
  590|       |    using container_t = typename std::remove_reference<CONTAINER>::type;
  591|       |    using raw_container_t = typename remove_ptr_type<container_t>::type;
  592|       |    using data_t = typename raw_container_t::value_type;
  593|       |    using container_fun_t = void(std::ostream &, data_t);
  594|       |    using fun_update_container_t = std::function<container_t(void)>;
  595|       |    using fun_filter_container_t = std::function<bool(const data_t&)>;
  596|       |    using fun_lock_container_t = std::function<std::shared_ptr<void>(const container_t&)>;
  597|       |
  598|       |    fun_update_container_t update_container_fun;
  599|       |    fun_filter_container_t filter_container_fun
  600|       |      = [](const data_t&){ return true; };
  601|       |    fun_lock_container_t lock_container_fun
  602|       |      = [](const container_t&){ return std::shared_ptr<void>{}; };
  603|       |
  604|       |    container_t current_rows;
  605|       |    FunctionSet<container_fun_t> container_funs;
  606|       |    emp::vector<std::string> container_keys;
  607|       |    emp::vector<std::string> container_descs;
  608|       |
  609|       |  public:
  610|       |    template <typename ...ARGS>
  611|       |    explicit ContainerDataFile(ARGS&& ...arguments)
  612|       |      : DataFile(std::forward<ARGS>(arguments)...), update_container_fun(), current_rows() {;}
  613|       |
  614|       |    ~ContainerDataFile() {;}
  615|       |
  616|       |    /// Tell this file what function to run to update the contents of the
  617|       |    /// container that data is being calculated on.
  618|       |    void SetUpdateContainerFun(const fun_update_container_t fun) {
  619|       |      update_container_fun = fun;
  620|       |    }
  621|       |
  622|       |    /// Tell this file what function to run to decide if a container entry
  623|       |    /// should be included in the data recorded.
  624|       |    void SetFilterContainerFun(const fun_filter_container_t fun) {
  625|       |      filter_container_fun = fun;
  626|       |    }
  627|       |
  628|       |    /// Tell this file what function to run to lock the container
  629|       |    /// before iterating through.
  630|       |    void SetLockContainerFun(const fun_lock_container_t fun) {
  631|       |      lock_container_fun = fun;
  632|       |    }
  633|       |
  634|       |    /// Tell this file what function to run to lock the container
  635|       |    /// before iterating through.
  636|       |    /// @Note convenience overload.
  637|       |    template<typename LockMaker>
  638|       |    void SetLockContainerFun(LockMaker lock_maker) {
  639|       |      SetLockContainerFun( fun_lock_container_t{ lock_maker } );
  640|       |    }
  641|       |
  642|       |    /// Print a header containing the name of each column
  643|       |    void PrintHeaderKeys() override {
  644|       |      *os << line_begin;
  645|       |      for (size_t i = 0; i < keys.size(); i++) {
  646|       |        if (i > 0) *os << line_spacer;
  647|       |        *os << keys[i];
  648|       |      }
  649|       |      for (size_t i = 0; i < container_keys.size(); i++) {
  650|       |        if (i > 0 || keys.size() > 0) *os << line_spacer;
  651|       |        *os << container_keys[i];
  652|       |      }
  653|       |      *os << line_end;
  654|       |      os->flush();
  655|       |    }
  656|       |
  657|       |    /// Print a header containing comments describing all of the columns
  658|       |    void PrintHeaderComment(const std::string & cstart = "# ") override {
  659|       |      for (size_t i = 0; i < keys.size(); i++) {
  660|       |        *os << cstart << i << ": " << descs[i] << " (" << keys[i] << ")" << std::endl;
  661|       |      }
  662|       |      for (size_t i = 0; i < container_keys.size(); i++) {
  663|       |        *os << cstart << i+keys.size() << ": " << container_descs[i] << " (" << container_keys[i] << ")" << std::endl;
  664|       |      }
  665|       |
  666|       |      os->flush();
  667|       |    }
  668|       |
  669|       |    const container_t GetCurrentRows() const { return current_rows; }
  670|       |
  671|       |    void FilterThenOutputLine(const data_t& d) {
  672|       |      if ( filter_container_fun(d) ) OutputLine(d);
  673|       |    }
  674|       |
  675|       |    void OutputLine(const data_t& d) {
  676|       |      *os << line_begin;
  677|       |        for (size_t i = 0; i < funs.size(); i++) {
  678|       |          if (i > 0) *os << line_spacer;
  679|       |          funs[i](*os);
  680|       |        }
  681|       |
  682|       |        for (size_t i = 0; i < container_funs.size(); i++) {
  683|       |          if (i > 0 || keys.size() > 0) *os << line_spacer;
  684|       |          container_funs[i](*os, d);
  685|       |        }
  686|       |      *os << line_end;
  687|       |    }
  688|       |
  689|       |    /// Run all of the functions and print the results as a new line in the file
  690|       |    void Update() override {
  691|       |      emp_assert(update_container_fun);
  692|       |      emp_assert(lock_container_fun);
  693|       |      current_rows = update_container_fun();
  694|       |      const auto lock{ lock_container_fun(current_rows) };
  695|       |      internal::update_impl<container_t>().Update(this);
  696|       |      os->flush();
  697|       |    }
  698|       |
  699|       |    /// Update the file with an additional set of lines.
  700|       |    void Update(size_t update) override {
  701|       |      if (timing_fun(update)) Update();
  702|       |    }
  703|       |
  704|       |    /// If a function takes an ostream, pass in the correct one.
  705|       |    /// Generic function for adding a column to the DataFile. In practice, you probably
  706|       |    /// want to call one of the more specific ones.
  707|       |    size_t Add(const std::function<void(std::ostream &, const data_t&)> & fun, const std::string & key, const std::string & desc) {
  708|       |      size_t id = container_funs.GetSize();
  709|       |      container_funs.Add(fun);
  710|       |      container_keys.emplace_back(key);
  711|       |      container_descs.emplace_back(desc);
  712|       |      return id;
  713|       |    }
  714|       |
  715|       |    /// Add a function that returns a value to be printed to the file.
  716|       |    template <typename T>
  717|       |    size_t AddContainerFun(const std::function<T(const data_t&)> & fun, const std::string & key="", const std::string & desc="") {
  718|       |      std::function<container_fun_t> in_fun = [fun](std::ostream & os, const data_t& data){ os << fun(data); };
  719|       |      return Add(in_fun, key, desc);
  720|       |    }
  721|       |
  722|       |    /// Add a function that returns a value to be printed to the file.
  723|       |    /// @note Convenience overload
  724|       |    template <typename Getter>
  725|       |    size_t AddContainerFun(Getter getter, const std::string & key="", const std::string & desc="") {
  726|       |      using ret_t = decltype(getter(std::declval<data_t>()));
  727|       |      return AddContainerFun(
  728|       |        std::function<ret_t(const data_t&)>{ getter }, key, desc
  729|       |      );
  730|       |    }
  731|       |
  732|       |
  733|       |  };
  734|       |
  735|       |  /// Convenience function for building a container data file.
  736|       |  /// @param fun is the function to call to update the container
  737|       |  /// @param filename is the name of the file to output to
  738|       |  /// @param b character to print at the beggininning of each line
  739|       |  /// @param s delimeter (i.e. what character to print between entries on each line)
  740|       |  /// @param e character to print at the end of each line
  741|       |  template <typename CONTAINER>
  742|       |  ContainerDataFile<CONTAINER> MakeContainerDataFile(std::function<CONTAINER(void)> fun,
  743|       |                                                    const std::string & filename,
  744|       |                                                    const std::string & b="",
  745|       |                                                    const std::string & s=",",
  746|       |                                                    const std::string & e="\n") {
  747|       |    ContainerDataFile<CONTAINER> dfile(filename, b, s, e);
  748|       |    dfile.SetUpdateContainerFun(fun);
  749|       |    return dfile;
  750|       |  }
  751|       |
  752|       |}
  753|       |
  754|       |#endif

   22|       |  /// A map of weighted indices.  If a random index is selected, the probability of an index being
   23|       |  /// returned is directly proportional to its weight.
   24|       |  class IndexMap {
   25|       |  private:
   26|       |    // Internally, item_weight should be thought of as following tree_weight as a vector, both of
   27|       |    // which are the length of the number of values stored.  Note that portions are mutable so
   28|       |    // that we can do a lazy updating of tree_weight when needs_refresh is set.
   29|       |
   30|       |    size_t num_items;                      ///< How many items are being stored in this IndexMap?
   31|       |    size_t zero_offset;                    ///< Position of id zero.
   32|       |    mutable bool needs_refresh;            ///< Are tree weights out of date?
   33|       |    mutable emp::vector<double> weights;   ///< The total weights in each sub-tree.
   34|       |
   35|       |    /// Which ID is the parent of the ID provided?
   36|      0|    size_t ParentID(size_t id) const { return (id-1) / 2; }
   37|       |
   38|       |    /// Which ID is the left child of the ID provided?
   39|      0|    size_t LeftID(size_t id) const { return 2*id + 1; }
   40|       |
   41|       |    /// Which ID is the right child of the ID provided?
   42|      0|    size_t RightID(size_t id) const { return 2*id + 2; }
   43|       |
   44|       |    /// Sift through the nodes to find the where index zero maps to.
   45|      0|    size_t CalcZeroOffset() const {
   46|      0|      size_t id = 0;
   47|      0|      while (id < num_items - 1) id = LeftID(id);
   48|      0|      return id - (num_items - 1);
   49|      0|    }
   50|       |
   51|      0|    size_t ToInternalID(size_t id) const {
   52|      0|      return (id + zero_offset) % num_items + num_items-1;
   53|      0|    }
   54|       |
   55|      0|    size_t ToInternalID(size_t id, size_t _items, size_t _offset) const {
   56|      0|      return (id + _offset) % _items + _items-1;
   57|      0|    }
   58|       |
   59|      0|    size_t ToExternalID(size_t id) const {
   60|      0|      return (id + 1 - zero_offset) % num_items;
   61|      0|    }
   62|       |
   63|       |    /// A Proxy class so that an index can be treated as an l-value.
   64|       |    class Proxy {
   65|       |    private:
   66|       |      IndexMap & index_map;  ///< Which index map is this proxy from?
   67|       |      size_t id;             ///< Which id does it represent?
   68|       |    public:
   69|      0|      Proxy(IndexMap & _im, size_t _id) : index_map(_im), id(_id) { ; }
   70|      0|      operator double() const { return index_map.RawWeight(id); }
   71|      0|      Proxy & operator=(double new_weight) { index_map.RawAdjust(id, new_weight); return *this; }
   72|       |    };
   73|       |
   74|       |    /// Check if we need to do a refresh, and if so do it!
   75|      0|    void ResolveRefresh() const {
   76|      0|      if (!needs_refresh) return;
   77|      0|
   78|      0|      // Internal nodes sum their two sub-trees.
   79|      0|      const size_t pivot = num_items - 1; // Transition between internal and leaf nodes.
   80|      0|      for (size_t i = pivot-1; i < pivot; i--) {
   81|      0|        weights[i] = weights[LeftID(i)] + weights[RightID(i)];
   82|      0|      }
   83|      0|
   84|      0|      needs_refresh = false;
   85|      0|    }
   86|       |
   87|       |  public:
   88|       |    /// Construct an IndexMap where num_items is the maximum number of items that can be placed
   89|       |    /// into the data structure.  All item weights default to zero.

   92|      0|    {

  120|      0|      if (new_size == num_items) return;                     // Already the right size?  Stop!
  121|      0|

  130|      0|      std::swap(weights, old_weights);

   21|       |  /// A vector of functions that can all be triggered at onece; results can either be returned
   22|       |  /// in a vector or post-processed in a function (such as max, min, etc.)
   23|       |  /// Derived from emp::vector, hence with all of the same methods as vector.
   24|       |  template <typename RETURN_T, typename... ARGS>
   25|       |  class FunctionSet<RETURN_T(ARGS...)> : public emp::vector<std::function<RETURN_T(ARGS...)>> {
   26|       |  protected:
   27|       |    mutable emp::vector<RETURN_T> return_vals;
   28|       |
   29|       |  public:
   30|       |    FunctionSet() : return_vals() { ; }
   31|       |    ~FunctionSet() { ; }
   32|       |
   33|       |    using base_t = emp::vector<std::function<RETURN_T(ARGS...)>>;
   34|       |    using value_type = typename base_t::value_type;
   35|       |    using return_t = RETURN_T;

   37|       |    /// How many functions are in this FunctionSet?

   44|       |    void Remove(size_t pos) { base_t::erase(base_t::begin()+pos); }
   45|       |
   46|       |    /// Run all functions and return a vector of all results.
   47|       |    const emp::vector<RETURN_T> & Run(ARGS... args) const {
   48|       |      const size_t num_tests = base_t::size();
   49|       |      return_vals.resize(num_tests);
   50|       |      for (size_t i = 0; i < num_tests; i++) {
   51|       |        return_vals[i] = (*this)[i](args...);
   52|       |      }
   53|       |      return return_vals;
   54|       |    }
   55|       |
   56|       |    /// If you want to provide a filter function, you can retrieve a specific return value.
   57|       |    /// The filter should take in two return values and indicate which is "better".
   58|       |    RETURN_T Run(ARGS... args, std::function<RETURN_T(RETURN_T, RETURN_T)> comp_fun,
   59|       |                 RETURN_T default_val=0) const {
   60|       |      if (base_t::size() == 0) return default_val;  // If we have no entries, return the default.
   61|       |
   62|       |      Run(args...);
   63|       |
   64|       |      RETURN_T best_found = return_vals[0];
   65|       |      for (size_t i = 1; i < return_vals.size(); i++) {
   66|       |        best_found = comp_fun(best_found, return_vals[i]);

   68|       |
   69|       |      return best_found;
   70|       |    }

  293|       |    /// @param alpha Shape parameter (default 1).

  295|       |    /// @param upper_bound Upper bound (default infinity, unbounded).
  296|       |    /// See <https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pareto_distribution>
  297|       |    inline double GetRandPareto(
  298|       |      const double alpha=1.0,
  299|       |      const double lower_bound=1.0,
  300|       |      const double upper_bound=std::numeric_limits<double>::infinity()
  301|  2.00k|    ) {
  302|  2.00k|      emp_assert( alpha > 0.0, alpha );
  303|  2.00k|      emp_assert( lower_bound > 0.0, lower_bound );
  304|  2.00k|      emp_assert( lower_bound <= upper_bound, lower_bound, upper_bound );
  305|  2.00k|      if (lower_bound == upper_bound) return lower_bound;

  307|       |      // see https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Inverse_transform_sampling
  308|       |      // and https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pareto_distribution
  309|       |
  310|       |      // see https://gist.github.com/mmore500/b6de0158a8851ce6e3d65105ed35197f
  311|       |      // for validation and testing

  313|       |

  315|  1.99k|      constexpr double unif_ub = 1.0;
  316|  1.99k|      const double unif_sample = GetDouble(unif_lb, unif_ub);
  317|       |
  318|  1.99k|      return lower_bound / std::pow(unif_sample, 1.0/alpha);
  319|  2.00k|    }

  337|       |    /// @return A random variable drawn from a distribution with symmetric
  338|       |    /// pareto tails extending both positively and negatively from zero.
  339|       |    /// @param alpha Shape parameter (default 1).
  340|       |    /// @param lambda Scale parameter (default 1).
  341|       |    /// @param lower_bound Lower bound (default -infinity, unbounded).
  342|       |    /// @param upper_bound Upper bound (default infinity, unbounded).
  343|       |    /// Inspired by <https://doi.org/10.1109/TBC.2004.834013>
  344|       |    inline double GetRandZeroSymmetricPareto(
  345|       |      const double alpha=1.0,
  346|       |      const double lambda=1.0,
  347|       |      const double lower_bound=-std::numeric_limits<double>::infinity(),
  348|       |      const double upper_bound=std::numeric_limits<double>::infinity()
  349|  1.00k|    ) {
  350|  1.00k|      emp_assert( alpha > 0.0, alpha );
  351|  1.00k|      emp_assert( lower_bound <= 0, lower_bound );

  353|       |
  354|       |      // see https://gist.github.com/mmore500/2b95ef3279e49fc1780b3b319c35083a
  355|       |      // for derivations and validation
  356|       |      // url has also been archived on the archive.org wayback machine
  357|       |
  358|      0|      const double n = std::min(std::abs(lower_bound), upper_bound);
  359|  1.00k|      const double m = std::max(std::abs(lower_bound), upper_bound);
  360|       |
  361|       |      // probability of picking on the skinny side of zero
  362|  1.00k|      const double p_skinny_side
  363|  1.00k|        = impl::calc_p_zero_symmetric_pareto_skinny_side(
  364|  1.00k|          alpha, lambda, m, n
  365|  1.00k|        );

  367|       |      // is the fat side of the distribution negative?
  368|  1.00k|      const bool skinny_side_is_positive = std::abs(lower_bound) > upper_bound;

  372|       |        // draw from positive side
  373|    479|        return GetRandLomax(alpha, lambda, upper_bound);
  374|    521|      } else {
  375|       |        // draw from negative side
  376|    521|        return -GetRandLomax(alpha, lambda, std::abs(lower_bound));
  377|    521|      }

  379|  1.00k|    }

  390|      0|    inline uint32_t GetRandPoisson(const double mean) {
  391|      0|      // Draw from a Poisson Dist with mean; if cannot calculate, return UINT_MAX.
  392|      0|      // Uses Rejection Method

  394|      0|      if (a <= 0) return UINT_MAX; // cannot calculate, so return UINT_MAX

  397|      0|      while (u >= a) {
  398|      0|        u *= GetDouble();
  399|      0|        ++k;
  400|      0|      }
  401|      0|      return k;
  402|      0|    }

  124|       |    T cur_result = Min(in2, extras...);
  125|       |    return (in1 < cur_result) ? in1 : cur_result;

  300|       |  template <typename T> const T & Min(std::initializer_list<const T&> lst) {
  301|       |    emp_assert(lst.size > 0); // Nothing to return if nothing in the list!
  302|       |    auto min_found = lst.begin();
  303|       |    for (auto it = lst.begin()+1; it < lst.end(); it++) {
  304|       |      if (*it < *min_found) min_found = it;
  305|       |    }
  306|       |    return *min_found;
  307|       |  }

   22|       | *

   24|       | *   the first time an ID is created this other static function will store the string associated
   25|       | *   with the idea to simplify later retrieval.  If called on a run-time string that it doesn't
   26|       | *   know it will also assign it a unique ID.  The one potential problem is if the run-time version
   27|       | *   is used BEFORE the compile time version.  As such, we need to always make sure to check if a

   59|       |namespace emp {
   60|       |
   61|       |  // Generic form of StringType (actual types will be made using specializations below)
   62|       |  template <char... Ts> struct StringType;
   63|       |
   64|       |  // Template specialization to maintain full information of a non-empty string as a type.
   65|       |  template <char C1, char... Cs>
   66|       |  struct StringType<C1,Cs...> {
   67|       |    static constexpr char FIRST = C1;             ///< Easy access to first character of string.
   68|       |    constexpr static int SIZE = 1+sizeof...(Cs);  ///< Easy access to string length.
   69|       |
   70|       |    using this_t = StringType<C1,Cs...>;          ///< The type of the current StringType
   71|       |    using pop = StringType<Cs...>;                ///< StringType after removing the first char
   72|       |    template <char C> using push = StringType<C, C1, Cs...>;       ///< Add char to front of string
   73|       |    template <char C> using push_back = StringType<C1, Cs..., C>;  ///< Add char to back of string
   74|       |
   75|       |    /// Does StringType contains the char C?
   76|       |    constexpr static bool Has(char C) { return (C==C1) | pop::Has(C); }
   77|       |
   78|       |    /// Count the number of occurances of char C in StringType.
   79|       |    constexpr static int Count(int C) { return pop::Count(C) + (C==C1); }
   80|       |
   81|       |    /// Determine the position at which C appears in StringType.
   82|       |    constexpr static int GetID(int C) {
   83|       |      if (C==C1) return 0;
   84|       |      if (!Has(C)) return -1;
   85|       |      return (1+pop::GetID(C));
   86|       |    }
   87|       |
   88|       |    /// Function to retrieve number of elements in StringType
   89|       |    constexpr static int GetSize() { return SIZE; }
   90|       |
   91|       |    /// Determine if there are NO chars in an StringType
   92|       |    constexpr static bool IsEmpty() { return false; }
   93|       |
   94|       |    /// Determine if all chars in StringType are different from each other.
   95|       |    constexpr static bool IsUnique() { return pop::IsUnique() && !pop::Has(C1); }
   96|       |
   97|       |    /// Convert this StringType back to an std::string object (note: NOT constexpr)
   98|       |    static std::string ToString() {
   99|       |      std::stringstream ss;
  100|       |      ss << C1;
  101|       |      (ss << ... << Cs);
  102|       |      return ss.str();
  103|       |    }
  104|       |  };
  105|       |
  106|       |  // Empty StringType template specialization
  107|       |  template <>
  108|       |  struct StringType<> {
  109|       |    static constexpr char FIRST = '\0';           ///< Empty string has null as "first" char
  110|       |    constexpr static int SIZE = 0;                ///< Empty string as no length
  111|       |
  112|       |    using this_t = StringType<>;                  ///< The type of the current StringType
  113|       |    // No pop_t; should give error if used on empty string
  114|       |    template <char C> using push = StringType<C>;       ///< Add char to front of string
  115|       |    template <char C> using push_back = StringType<C>;  ///< Add char to back of string
  116|       |
  117|       |    /// Empty StringType does not contain the char C.
  118|      0|    constexpr static bool Has(char C) { return false; }
  119|       |
  120|       |    /// Empty StringType has 0 occurances of anything.
  121|      0|    constexpr static int Count(int C) { return 0; }
  122|       |

  126|       |    /// Empty StringType has size 0.
  127|      0|    constexpr static int GetSize() { return 0; }
  128|       |

  130|      0|    constexpr static bool IsEmpty() { return true; }
  131|       |
  132|       |    /// Empty StringType is always unique.
  133|      0|    constexpr static bool IsUnique() { return true; }
  134|       |  };

  136|      0|  constexpr size_t CalcStringSize(const char * in) {
  137|      0|    size_t count = 0;
  138|      0|    while (in[count] != 0) count++;
  139|      0|    return count;
  140|      0|  }

  144|       |  constexpr auto StringPacketToStringType(T packet) {

  146|       |    else {

    6|       |//
    7|       |//
    8|       |//  Developer notes:
    9|       |//  * Fill out defaults for remaining standard library classes (as possible)
   10|       |//  * If a class has a static TypeID_GetName() defined, use that for the name.
   11|       |//  * If a type is a template, give access to parameter types.
   12|       |//  * If a type is a function, give access to parameter types.
   13|       |

   15|       |#define EMP_TYPE_ID_H
